いが、好調なAI関連輸出が景気を下支え。

資ともに押し上げ。日銀は物価上振れリ
スクを意識し利上げを前倒し
内需が力強さを欠くなか、強固な製造能
力を背景とした輸出攻勢が経済を支える

ん引。消費も減税や資産効果を受け底堅

投資等が下支え。先行きもAI・レジリエン

原油だけでなく石油精製品や天然ガスなどにも供給制約。インフレ圧力は長期化
◼ 原油高に加え、製油能力逼迫に伴うクラック・スプレッドの上昇もグローバルなインフレを助長する懸念
― クラック・スプレッド（石油精製品と原油の価格差）は例年を大きく上回る水準。戦争の影響を背景に中東・ロシアからの
輸出が減少していること等が影響
◼ 代替ルートの乏しい天然ガスや肥料（尿素など）の供給懸念は継続。先物価格は高止まり
― 天然ガスでは中東依存度が高い欧州やアジアで価格が上昇。欧州ガス価格（TTF）は前年比で約2.4倍まで急騰

― 先物価格も需給ひっ迫継続を見越して高止まり。中東情勢次第では、天然ガス価格がさらに上振れる可能性も

強まる投資主導型経済の様相。拡大するAI・レジリエンス投資が世界経済の成長をけん引

（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

◼ 原油供給回復の遅れ
◼ 石油精製能力の逼迫
◼ 天然ガス・肥料の供給制約
AI・レジリエンス投資の拡大
◼ 設備・資材需要の増加
◼ 電力・人材需要の増加
◼ 資金需要の拡大

インフレ長期化：エネルギー供給ショックや投資需要がインフレ圧力に
◼ ホルムズ海峡の封鎖長期化により、エネルギー価格は当面高止まり
― 開戦以降、グローバルCPIのエネルギー指数は前年比約15％まで上昇。原油や石化製品などの高騰が、航空運賃など
のサービス価格や、輸送コストの上昇を通じた幅広い財価格に波及
◼ 旺盛なAI関連需要が物価を押し上げ
― データセンター投資需要を受けメモリー価格が高騰。PC・スマホ等の最終製品価格へも波及
― 需要拡大に伴う電力価格の上昇も相まって、供給ショックと投資需要が同時に物価を押し上げ

（注） 政府機関閉鎖による米CPIデータ欠損のため、2025年10月は欠値

高金利の常態化：政策金利に加え、タームプレミアムも金利を押し上げ
◼ 主要国・地域の中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ移行
― ホルムズ危機長期化や投資需要の強さに伴うインフレ圧力の高まりを受け、日米欧の中銀はいずれも9月に利上げ
◼ 日本以外の主要国においても、政策金利の上昇織り込みや財政懸念を背景に長期金利は上昇
― 米国では財政懸念に加え、足元では政策金利の上昇織り込みが長期金利を押し上げ

### Page 13

成長・負担の偏在が広げる「耐性格差」―4つの緊張関係
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
②企業・家計間の緊張
生産性上昇
企業収益増加
遅れる賃金
・所得への波及
③企業間の緊張
大企業
／AI・戦略分野
中小企業
／非AI・戦略分野
④家計間の緊張
資産保有層
／高所得層
資産非保有層
／中低所得層
①国家間の緊張
技術／資本／資源
を持つ国
技術／資本／資源
を持たない国
→
成長の実感乏しく、社会的受容性が低下
→
保護主義・産業政策競争が加速
→
消費・資産形成力の差が累積
→
投資余力の差が次の成長機会を左右
格差は一時的か、固定化・自己増幅するか
（耐性格差が強まる中での日本の立ち位置は？）
投資主導経済＋インフレ＋高金利 拡大する耐性格差成長・負担の偏在
偏在化する成長の果実「新常態」下の金融市場中東危機長期化、加速するAI・レジリエンス投資

### Page 14

「耐性格差」は固定化するのか―成長の持続性を見極める3つの評価軸
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
見極める軸 ポイント 代表指標 含意
波及 成長の果実は広がるか
◼ 生産性
◼ 実質賃金
◼ AI導入率
◼ 中小企業収益
波及すれば成長の裾野が拡大
しなければ成長の偏りが固定化
機会 「持たざる側」も次の成長機
会に参加できるか
◼ 労働移動
◼ 新規参入
◼ 資金調達格差
◼ 人的資本投資
参加できれば格差は流動的
できなければ勝者・敗者が固定化
底上げ 格差が残る中でも、弱い主
体の生活水準は改善するか
◼ ジニ係数（再分配）
◼ リスキリング
◼ 資産形成
◼ 社会移動
改善すれば社会的受容性を維持
しなければ分断圧力が強まる
問われるのは格差そのものではなく、偏在が成長の持続性を損なうか
⇒成長の果実が広がり、次の機会と生活水準の改善につながるかが焦点
評
価
軸
と
そ
の
含
意
問
題
設
定
「波及・機会・底上げ」を通じて、成長の持続性をモニタリング
偏在化する成長の果実「新常態」下の金融市場中東危機長期化、加速するAI・レジリエンス投資

### Page 15

参考①（国家間）：競争力の基盤は国ごとに偏在、資源高は日本に逆風
◼ 国家の競争力は、資源・製造能力・技術力・資金動員力・市場規模など複数の基盤に左右
― 日本は製造能力・技術力に強みを持つ一方、資源面の脆弱性から資源高局面では交易条件が悪化。また、潤沢な資金
（金融資産）を有しているものの、国内成長分野への資金動員力に課題
― 台湾・韓国では資源高で輸入価格が上昇する一方、半導体などAI関連財の価格上昇が輸出価格を押し上げ、交易条件
を下支え
国家の競争力を支える5つの基盤

参考②（企業・家計間）：生産性向上に遅れる賃金、低下する労働分配率
◼ 日本では時間当たり生産性が上昇する一方、1人当たり実質賃金の伸びは限定的
― 働き方改革など労働時間の短縮も両者の乖離に影響。交易条件悪化による所得流出も実質賃金を下押しする一因に
― 対して、AIブームの恩恵が大きい米国や韓国では、労働生産性と実質賃金がともに上昇する好循環

◼ 日本の労働分配率は主要国・地域と比較しても低下傾向。企業収益の賃金への還元が今後の焦点
― 米国や韓国においても足元で低下がみられるが、AI需要の拡大による企業収益の伸びが主因
― 日本では雇用者報酬の伸びが緩やかであり、生産性向上の成果を賃金に繋げられるかどうかが課題
（注） 労働生産性は総労働時間あたりの実質GDP、実質賃金は雇用者1人あたり
（出所） OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
労働生産性と実質賃金 主要国・地域における労働分配率の推移
（注） 自営業者の労働所得を調整した雇用者報酬の名目GDP比。韓国の2024年以降は
OECDのデータを用いて延長
（出所） 欧州委員会、OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 17

参考③（企業間）：金利上昇への耐性は企業規模・業種で大きな格差
◼ 日本企業の投下資本利益率（ROIC）は規模・業種間で大きな格差
― 総じてみれば、企業規模が小さいほどROICが低い傾向（中堅企業は例外）。特に小規模企業は1.5％と低水準
― 業種別では、製造業の輸送用機械や、非製造業の不動産、電力・ガス、運輸・郵便、医療・福祉などでROICが低い傾向。
なお、情報通信や宿泊・飲食は大企業のROICが相対的に高い一方、小規模企業が低く、業種内の格差が大きい
◼ インタレストカバレッジレシオ（ICR）も規模・業種間で格差。ROIC・ICRともに低い規模・業種ほど金利上昇に脆弱な構造
― ICRも規模が小さいほど低位（中堅企業は例外）。業種別では、不動産、電力・ガス、運輸・郵便、医療・福祉等が低水準
（注） 投下資本利益率（ROIC）＝税引き後営業利益÷（有利子負債＋自己資本）。大企業
は資本金10億円以上、中堅企業は1億円以上10億円未満、中小企業は1千万円以上
1億円未満、小規模企業は1千万円未満

（出所） 財務省「法人企業統計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（注） インタレストカバレッジレシオ（ICR）＝営業利益÷支払利息。大企業は資本金10億円

低所得世帯 高所得世帯
参考④（家計間）：金融資産を保有する層と持たざる層の間でインフレ耐性に格差
◼ 所得階級によって資産形成余力に格差。低所得世帯ほど資産形成が進みにくく、インフレ耐性が脆弱
― 高所得世帯では貯蓄余力が大きい一方、低所得世帯では物価高による生活費の増加が重石となり、貯蓄率は低水準
― 低所得世帯では金融資産の蓄積が進みにくく、インフレによる実質所得の目減りを資産所得で補う余地も限定的
◼ 資産形成余力に加え、資産選択も二極化。インフレ下では資産格差が拡大し、インフレ耐性の差も広がりやすい構造
― 余裕資金のある高所得世帯は収益性重視のリスク資産投資に積極的。一方、中低所得世帯はリスク資産投資に消極的
― インフレは資産形成余力と資産選択の違いを増幅させ、資産格差とインフレ耐性の格差がさらに拡大する可能性
（注） 勤労者世帯の平均貯蓄率。平均貯蓄率＝貯蓄純増 ÷ 可処分所得 × 100。対象

世帯・項目の違いにより、GDPベースの貯蓄率とは一致しない
（出所） 総務省「家計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
所得別にみた貯蓄率（2021～25年平均） 年収別にみた金融資産の選択基準（2021～25年）
（注） 対象は金融資産を保有する二人以上世帯
（出所） J-FLEC「家計の金融行動に関する世論調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（１）米国：AIによる景気押し上げは当面継続。物価高から FRBは一時的に政策金利を引き上げへ
◼ 2026・27年の実質GDP成長率はいずれも前年比＋2.2％と予想
― 設備投資は、2027年入り後に巡航速度並みの伸び率へ回帰。ハイパースケーラーは投資効率の低下を意識し、AIの普
及ペースを見極めながら投資を継続
― 個人消費は減税政策や資産効果により底堅く推移。2027年入り後はインフレ減速に伴う実質所得の改善も支えに
◼ FRBは関税引き上げや原油高によるインフレ上振れリスクを警戒し、2026年内に追加で1回の利上げ。関税・原油高の影
響が一巡した後、 物価の十分な減速が確認される2027年後半以降に2回の利下げを予想

◼ 足元の景気は堅調。引き続き、AIを中心とする設備投資が景気のけん引役に

◼ 2026年後半は、熱波を受けた生産抑制やインフレによる消費下押しなど逆風はありつつも、財政拡張が下支え

### Page 32

欧州：熱波による河川水位低下が景気下押し要因に
◼ 河川の水位低下による生産下振れが7～9月期の成長率を下押し
― 高温と少雨を背景に欧州各地では河川の水位が低下。内陸物流において重要な役割を担うライン川の一部地点では、6
月以降、船舶の積載量に制約が生じる水位が継続
― ライン川の水位がおおよそ100㎝を切ったあたりから10㎝低下するごとにドイツの生産が0.4％程度減少すると試算
― 実際、船舶の輸送機能低下による原材料の供給制約を受けて化学・鉄鋼等の一部素材業種では生産抑制の動き。7～
9月期の経済下押し要因に
ライン川の水位とドイツ鉱工業生産の関係
（注） 閾値モデルを用いてライン川の水位がドイツ鉱工業生産に与える影響を推計。縦軸
の鉱工業生産は景気要因をコントロール（ドイツ鉱工業生産を世界の鉱工業生産に

◼ 天然ガスや農作物価格上昇の影響は今年後半から来年のインフレ押し上げ要因に
― 熱波による発電所の停止や農作物の不作懸念等を背景に、欧州の天然ガス・農産物の先物価格は上昇。これらの価格
上昇がインフレに与える影響は、先行き1年程度残存する計算

― 需給が緩和するにつれ、エネルギー・食料品そのものによる物価押し上げ幅は縮小へ。ただし、インフレ期待の上昇を招

### Page 36

（３）新興国：中国は景気減速が継続。アジアは原油調達の安定や AIブームが追い風に
◼ 中国は景気の減速傾向が続く見通し。2026年の成長率は4.3％、2027年は3.8％まで低下すると予想
― 内需は力強さを欠く。消費は補助金の反動で低迷が継続。投資も過当競争や地方財政難を受けて振るわず
― 外需は景気を下支え。EVなどの製品で輸出攻勢が続き、純輸出がプラスに寄与
◼ アジアでは原油の調達不安が後退、AIブームが景気をけん引。一方、インフレ・金利上昇圧力の高まりには留意が必要
― 原油調達は、代替輸入やバイオ燃料等の活用で懸念が後退。AIブームによる財輸出や域内投資のけん引が継続
― 他方、原油高の再燃やAIブームの継続がインフレ圧力を高める構図。金融政策は引き締め方向へ緩やかにシフト

中国：政府目標は達成圏内だが成長の勢いは弱い。内需が力強さを欠くなか、デフレ圧力は継続
◼ 2026年上期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.7％。政府目標（＋4.5％～5.0％）の範囲内だが、内需の弱さが目立つ結果
― 4～6月期は同＋4.3％と2022年Q4（＋3.0％）以来の低水準。中東情勢を受けた供給ショックもあり、最終消費と総資本
形成が前期から減速。内需の弱さを純輸出（外需）が補う構図は変わらず

中国：原油輸入量は大幅減も、依然供給不足には至らず。代替燃料を用いた生産も拡大
◼ ホルムズ海峡の封鎖により、原油の輸入量は2ケタ減が続く。ただ、需要の低迷もあり供給不足には陥らず
― 海上輸送による原油輸入（3～9月）は封鎖前（10月～2月）対比▲45.0％。ロシアからの代替輸入も限定的
― ガソリンの消費量は3～8月に前年比▲8.8％、ディーゼルは同▲10.7％と低迷しており、需給のひっ迫を回避

中国：消費・投資の低迷が続く一方、輸出と生産は堅調。外需に成長を依存する構図が継続
◼ 消費・投資は減速に歯止めかからず。内需は一段と低迷
― 投資は前年比▲10％超の減少が継続。不動産不況、過当競争、地方財政難によるインフラ投資減速が背景
― 小売も減速傾向が継続。補助金の反動による自動車販売の減少等が下押し
― 一方、生産は引き続き底堅く推移。堅調な輸出を追い風に、コンピューター・通信機器などを中心に高い伸び継続
◼ 輸出はAIブームと自動車の輸出攻勢が継続。低迷する内需を外需の伸びで支える構図
― 輸出拡大は産業政策による高度化の成果と言える一方、貿易不均衡の拡大に対する懸念も（後述）

中国：好調な輸出の一方で輸入は停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
◼ 輸入は2010年代中盤以降に頭打ち。「自立自強」による輸入代替の進展が背景にあると考えられる
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアは、輸出がほぼ一貫して上昇している一方、輸入は2010年代中盤以降に頭打ち
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小
― 輸出伸長、輸入停滞の結果、貿易不均衡が拡大。先進各国はこれに懸念表明。貿易摩擦が再び強まるおそれも

アジア：原油高とAIブーム影響で生産コストが膨張。消費者価格への転嫁でインフレ圧力が高まる
◼ 企業の生産コストは増大。原油高を受けてエネルギーや石油由来製品の価格が急騰するほか、AIブーム下のメモリ高騰を
背景に、コンピューターや電気機器類の価格にも上昇圧力
― 主要国の生産者物価は総じて上昇ペースが加速。石油や有機化学製品、コンピューター類の品目が押し上げ
◼ 生産コストは消費者価格に波及。原油高がエネルギー費目を押し上げるほか、スマホやPCの価格も一部でインフレ要因に

― 幅広い地域でエネルギー部門がインフレ率を押し上げ。ガソリンや燃料（LPガス等）のほか、航空運賃の上昇が寄与
― 寄与度は小さいものの、スマホやPCなどの電子機器価格も上昇。メモリ価格の高騰が最終品価格に波及している模様
消費者物価：原油高・AIブーム影響

◼ インフレ圧力と通貨安のもと、金融政策は引き締めにシフト。ただし、内需下押し懸念から政策スタンスは域内でまちまちに

（４）日本：26年度は中東情勢影響のなかでも堅調に推移。 27年度は政策が消費・投資を押し上げ
◼ 2026年度の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋0.8％と予測。中東情勢緊迫化の影響を受ける一方、AI関連需要が下支え
― 中東情勢緊迫化は、原油価格上昇による交易利得・損失の悪化（実質的な所得の海外流出）や先行き不透明感の高ま
りを通じ、消費・投資を抑制。一方、AI・半導体関連輸出の拡大や投資促進税制の開始を受け、堅調な成長に
◼ 2027年度は同＋1.3％と予測。消費税減税の開始による個人消費の押し上げに加え、国内投資拡大がけん引

― 消費税減税が物価上昇の抑制を通じて実質賃金の改善要因に。また、政府の「強く豊かな日本投資枠」による戦略17分
野への支援開始に伴い、設備投資の拡大が成長率を押し上げると予想
（注） 網掛けは予測値

### Page 49

日本：コアCPIは2026年度末にかけて一時前年比＋3％程度に。日銀は2％まで利上げ継続
◼ AIブームの恩恵を受け、企業業績は中東情勢影響のなかでも改善傾向を維持
― 企業の好業績や人手不足の強さを背景に、2027年度の春闘でも5％程度の高い賃上げ率が続くと予想
◼ コアCPI（生鮮食品を除く総合）は、2026年度末にかけて一時的に前年比＋3％程度へ加速
― 原油価格の上昇が消費者物価を押し上げ。物価上振れリスクに対応するため、日銀は2％まで追加利上げを実施へ
― 2027年度に入ると、原油価格が徐々に落ち着くほか、利上げがインフレ率の上昇を抑制。加えて消費税減税が物価を一
段と下押しし、コアCPIが一時的に前年比＋1％台半ばへ低下する見込み
（注） 1. 網掛けは予測値。変化率は、水準のデータからみずほ総合研究所調査部が計算した値であり、公表値と一致しないことがある
2. 経常利益は法人企業統計調査の全規模・全産業（除く金融・保険）ベース
3. 消費者物価の「除く制度要因」は、政府による電気・ガス料金支援、ガソリン補助・暫定税率廃止、高校授業料無償化、消費税減税等の影響を除いて計算

日本：2026年度の成長率を上方修正。AI関連を中心とする輸出の堅調さが主な要因
◼ 2026年度の実質GDP成長率（＋0.8％）は、前回6月見通し（＋0.5％）対比で0.3％Ptの上方修正
― 米国を中心とする旺盛なAI関連需要の恩恵を受け、財輸出が堅調に拡大するとの見方が、26年度上方修正の主因
― 輸入価格上昇を受けた交易利得・損失の悪化（実質的な所得の海外流出）は、個人消費や設備投資などの抑制要因と
して残るものの、輸出の堅調な拡大が景気下押し圧力を一部緩和
◼ 2027年度は、消費税減税の開始や投資促進政策の効果発現により、引き続き高い成長を予想
― ただし、インフレ率の上振れを受けて実質所得の伸びがやや弱まることなどから、前回6月対比で小幅に下方修正

日本：政府が掲げる「2040年度：250兆円」の国内投資目標は、概ね実現可能な範囲
◼ 投資促進税制（26～28年度）に加え、戦略17分野を中心として「強く豊かな日本投資枠」（27～40年度）による支援を実施
― 「強く豊かな日本投資枠」は、27年度概算要求ベース（一般・特別会計）で14.2兆円と大規模
◼ 設備投資は自然体でも2040年度に230兆円へ到達する計算。政府目標は概ね現実的な範囲
― 直近実績を踏まえ「自然体」の伸びを＋4％とすると、目標達成に必要な押し上げ分は累計125兆円（年平均8兆円強）。

日本：輸入増の影響を考慮しても、投資拡大が名目GDPの水準を＋0.5％程度押し上げ
◼ 日本の資本財供給では輸入浸透度が上昇。グローバルな資本財需給ひっ迫は国内供給力強化の足かせに
― とりわけ、電気機械・情報通信機械では輸入依存度の高まりが顕著
― 半導体等を中心にグローバルな需給ひっ迫が続くとみられ、AI・半導体分野等を中心に投資完了には時間
◼ もっとも、輸入増による需要の海外漏出を考慮しても、国内生産増を通じたGDP押し上げ効果は相応の規模
― 投資需要が期待通り実現すれば、投資促進策がない場合と比べて名目GDP水準を平均＋0.5％程度押し上げ

◼ 国内投資の政府支援策により、設備投資額の前年比変化率が2026～40年度に0.1～0.9％Pt程度押し上げられると予想

― 資本投入拡大に加え、R&D、AI実装、企業の新陳代謝などが押し上げ。さらなる改善には規制緩和などの改革が必要

日本：原油の代替調達は継続。ただし、油価再上昇や輸送コスト増加が物価上振れ要因に
◼ 今後も原油の代替調達が継続する見通し。時期により調達先が変化するものの、原油の供給不足は回避

日本：コスト高を受け、コアCPIは26年度末にかけて加速。27年度は消費税減税が下押し要因に

― 原油高による前年比押し上げの縮小や、日銀の利上げ効果、円安一服による輸入コスト上昇圧力の緩和が影響

◼ 2026年度後半はインフレ加速により、実質賃金の伸びが鈍化。個人消費は緩慢な回復に留まる見通し
― 個人消費は、良好な所得環境にもかかわらず低調。背景には、中東情勢の悪化に伴う消費者マインドの弱含み

金融政策：インフレ圧力の下で価格転嫁行動が積極化。日銀は 27年央に2％まで利上げ
◼ コスト高・供給不安定化のもと、日本企業の価格転嫁行動に積極化の兆候
― 民間調査では幅広い業種における価格転嫁短期化が示されているほか、足元では価格転嫁の動きに広がり。値上げ行
動の積極化は、収益改善を支えるとともに、物価上昇圧力の定着に繋がる可能性
◼ 物価上振れ警戒モードの金融政策運営が継続。9月会合後も日銀は短いインターバルで利上げ
― 9月会合後の会見で植田総裁は、物価引き上げから物価上振れ抑制へと「政策の局面が変化した」と説明

― 27年央にかけ四半期に1回程度のペースで利上げ。堅調な資金需要・投資計画を踏まえ、ターミナルレートは2％と予想
（注） 品目別に前年比の伸びを計算し、3カ月前から加速した品目の割合から減速した品
目の割合を差し引いて算出した指数

長期金利：市場の政策金利織り込みは2％に収束へ。長期金利は3％超の水準で高止まり
◼ 2026年度の長期金利は、物価上振れ懸念と利上げ織り込みの進展を背景に上昇
― タームプレミアム（上乗せ金利）は物価上振れ懸念から夏場にかけて大きく拡大したが、その後は緩やかな拡大ペースに
◼ 2027年度以降の長期金利は横ばい圏で推移。3％超の水準が常態化する見通し
― 物価上振れ懸念が後退するもとで、行き過ぎた利上げ織り込みは徐々に縮小
― 一方、日銀のバランスシート縮小を背景にイールドカーブの正常化が進展。日銀の大規模な国債保有で抑制されていた
タームプレミアムが緩やかに再拡大し、利上げ織り込みのはく落による金利低下圧力をおおむね相殺

（注） 政策金利織り込みは、今後10年間の政策金利に対する市場織り込みの平均。網掛け
部は期末政策金利との差分。2026年Q2までNakajima (2025)による推計値、2026年
Q3期以降みずほ総研推計値
（出所） Nakajima, J. (2025). “Impact of US monetary policy spillovers and yield curve
control policy,”、一上響・上野陽一 (2013). 「ゼロ金利下におけるタームプレミアム
の推計：日米英の長期金利の分析」、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

為替：ドル円レートのレンジは2022年頃に円安方向へシフト。今後も構造的な円安圧力が残存
◼ 長い目でみれば、ドル円レートは2022年ごろを境にレンジが円安方向にシフト
― 2000～21年のドル円のレンジは1ドル＝80円～120円程度だったが、2022年以降は1ドル＝130円～170円程度に
― 背景には、①高い米金利、②円の需給環境の悪化、③日本のインフレノルムの定着など
◼ 先行きも、1ドル＝150円程度を中心とした円安水準が継続しやすい経済環境に
― 27年度は、短期的には「米利下げ」＋「日銀利上げ」で日米金利差が縮小し、円高要因に
― ただし、原油高が続くもとでの日本の貿易赤字、期待インフレの高止まり等が円安要因となり、大幅円高は見込まず
（出所） LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

需給

• 原油高長期化で、輸入は高止まり
• デジタル赤字は拡大へ
• 対外直接投資も増加基調継続へ

• 期待インフレ率の高止まりは、購買

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移(年度）(前年比）
(資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
(年度）
予測

＜実質成長率：2026 年度 0.9％、2027 年度 1.3％を予想＞

１. 2026 年 4-6 月期の実質 GDP（2 次速報値）は、設備投資の上方修正などから、1 次速報
の前期比 0.3％（年率 1.1％）から前期比 0.4％（年率 1.4％）に上方修正された。 
２. GDP2 次速報の結果を受けて、8 月に発表した経済見通しを改定した。実質 GDP 成長率
は 2026 年度が 0.9％、2027 年度が 1.3％と予想する。2026 年 4-6 月期の実績値が上振れ
たことを受けて、2026 年度の見通しを 0.1％上方修正した。 
３. 景気は、物価上昇ペースの加速に伴う実質購買力の低下を背景に民間消費が低迷するこ
とを主因に、2026 年度末にかけて停滞色が強まるだろう。 
４. 2027 年度に入ると、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下から家計の実
質購買力が改善し、民間消費が回復に向かうほか、原材料価格の低下に伴う企業収益の
改善を受けて設備投資の増勢ペースが加速することが見込まれる。 
５. 消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品を除く総合）は、輸入物価の大幅上昇が食料品や日用品等
の幅広い品目に波及し、2026 年度末には 3％台まで加速するが、飲食料品の消費税率引
き下げを受けて、2027 年度には 1％台まで鈍化するだろう。消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食
品を除く総合）は、2026 年度が 2.3％、2027 年度が 1.3％と予想する。

2026・2027 年度経済見通し 
－26 年 4-6 月期ＧＤＰ2 次速報後改定

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-09-08

### Page 2

2026 年 4-6 月期の GDP 統計は、複数の特殊要因（高校授業料・小学校給食費の無償化1、特許権
の海外譲渡2、国家備蓄原油の放出3）が需要項目を大きく変動させており、個々の需要項目から景
気の基調を読み取りにくい結果となった。 ただし、 中東情勢の悪化による下押し圧力を受けながら
も、 実質GDP が 0％台半ばから後半とされる潜在成長率を上回る伸びを確保したことは、 景気の底
堅さを示すものとして一定の評価ができるだろう。

（経常利益は過去最高の更新が続く） 
財務省の法人企業統計によれば、2026 年 4-6 月期の全産業（金融業、保険業を除く、以下同
じ）の経常利益は前年比 24.6％（1-3 月期：同 14.6％）と 7 四半期連続の増益となり、前期から伸
びが高まった。非製造業が前年比 9.8％（1-3 月期：同 1.4％）と 22 四半期連続の増加、製造業が
前年比 57.0％（1-3 月期：同 42.9％）と 4 四半期連続の増加となり、いずれも増加ペースが前期か
ら加速した。 
季節調整済の経常利益は前期比 11.0％（1-3 月期：同 5.9％）と 4 四半期連続で増加した。製造
業（前期比 12.1％） 、非製造業（同10.2％）ともに前期比二桁の高い伸びとなった。製造業は 4 四
半期連続で前期比二桁の伸びとなった。 
経常利益（季節調整値）は 37.7 兆円（1-3 月
期：34.0 兆円）と 4 四半期連続で過去最高水
準を更新した。製造業、非製造業ともに過去
最高となった。 
2026 年 4-6 月期は、中東情勢の悪化を受け
たエネルギーの供給制約、物流の停滞、原油
価格高騰等が企業収益を下押しすることが懸
念されていた。しかし、実際には原油価格上
昇の影響で製造業、非製造業ともに変動費は

1 家計消費から政府消費への振り替えが生じたため、家計消費が押し下げられる一方、政府消費が押し上げられた。 
2 サービス輸出を押し上げる一方、設備投資（研究開発）を押し下げた。 
3 原油輸入量の急減が成長率を押し上げる一方、原油の調達難を受けた国家備蓄原油の放出により、公的在庫変動が大
幅なマイナス寄与となった。

### Page 3

（実質賃金上昇率は 2026 年度末にかけていったんマイナスへ） 
名目賃金を消費者物価 （持家の帰属家賃を除く総合） で割り引いた実質賃金は、2026 年 1 月に 13
ヵ月ぶりのプラスとなった後、7 ヵ月連続でプラスの伸びを維持している。名目賃金（現金給与総
額）が 2025 年中の 2％台前半から 2026 年入り後には 3％台へ伸びを高めたことに加え、政府によ
るエネルギー負担緩和策の影響などから、消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）の上昇率が大
きく縮小したことが実質賃金の押し上げに寄与している。 
実質賃金上昇率は、 消費者物価の上昇ペース加速を受けて伸び率が徐々に鈍化し、2026 年度末に
かけていったんマイナスに転じるだろう。 しかし、2027 年度入り後には、飲食料品の消費税率引き
下げを受けて消費者物価上昇率 が鈍化することを主因として 再びプラスに転じることが予想され
る。

２. 実質成長率は 2026 年度 0.9％、2027 年度 1.3％を予想 
2026 年 4-6 月期の GDP2 次速報を受けて、8/18 に発表した経済見通しを改定した。実質 GDP 成
長率は 2026 年度が 0.9％、2027 年度が 1.3％と予想する。2026 年 4-6 月期の実績値が方修正された
がことを受けて、2026 年度の見通しを 0.1％上方修正した。

日本経済は、2025 年 10-12 月期から 2026 年 4-6 月期まで 3 四半期連続で 0％台半ばから後半とさ
れる潜在成長率を上回る成長となったが、 先行きは回復ペースが鈍化することが予想される。 消費
者物価の川上段階に当たる企業物価はすでに大きく上昇しており、 先行きは食料品や日用品など幅
広い品目で値上げの動きが広がり、 消費者物価の上昇ペースは再加速することが見込まれる。 物価
上昇に伴う実質購買力の低下を主因として民間消費は低迷が続き、2027 年 1-3 月期は飲食料品の消

（物価の見通し） 
消費者物価（生鮮食品を除く総合、以下コアCPI）は、2026 年 1 月に前年比 1.9％と 2022 年 3 月
以来、3 年 10 ヵ月ぶりの 2％割れとなった後、7 月まで 7 ヵ月連続で 1％台の伸びとなっている。
消費者物価上昇率鈍化の理由は、既往の原油価格下落に加え、ガソリンの暫定税率廃止、電気・ガ
ス代の支援策などからエネルギー価格が下落している
こと、2025 年夏場以降、食料の上昇率鈍化が続いてい
ることである。 
ただし、 消費者物価の川上段階に位置する飲食料品の
輸入物価はすでに上昇率が高まっており、 今後、 川下の
消費者物価の食料品に波及することが見込まれる。

響を受けた原油価格の上昇や円安の進展を反映し、 輸入

Document 148: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm sâu trong tuần qua, 57.87 điểm tương ứng -3.12%, với thanh khoản 
suy giảm rõ rệt. Đồ gia dụng, Hàng xa xỉ & dệt may, Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức 
giảm rõ nét nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại tiếp tục động thái bán ròng so với xu hướng của 
tuần trước với giá trị -1,634 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VPB, CTG và VCB; đồng thời, tự 
doanh duy trì bán ròng ở mức -317 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng 3,421 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,992 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 1,432 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 199 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần điều chỉnh đầu tiên sau 3 tuần tăng liên tiếp khi VN-Index đánh 
mất mốc tâm lý 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.795 điểm. Áp lực chốt lời sau nhịp 
hồi tích cực trong tháng 8, cộng hưởng với rủi ro căng thẳng Trung Đông leo thang là 2 
nguyên nhân chính khiến áp lực bán tăng mạnh. Nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng 4/5 
phiên trong tuần qua cho thấy dòng vốn ngoại vẫn dè dặt ngay trước thời điểm nâng hạng 
— làm dấy lên lo ngại kịch bản "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" quanh ngày 21/9. Nhìn chung, 
tâm lý thị trường chung có sự chuyển biến thận trọng trước các sự kiện quan trọng sắp 
diễn ra: diễn biến giá dầu và địa chính trị Trung Đông, cuộc họp chính sách của Fed giữa 
tháng 9, và thời điểm Việt Nam chính thức được FTSE nâng hạng lên thị trường mới nổi 
thứ cấp vào 21/9.

CTCP Tập đoàn Gemadept (GMD)  
Duy trì tăng trưởng bền vững 
03/09/2026   
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tiếp tục 
tăng trưởng tích cực

Sản lượng hàng qua cảng GMD tiếp 
tục duy trì đà tăng trưởng bền vững

Giá dịch vụ kỳ vọng duy trì đà tăng 
ổn định từ nay đến 2030

Khuyến ngh ị MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
92,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu thuần của GMD 2Q2026 đạt 1,762 tỷ VND (+18% yoy). Tổng sản lượng qua 
hệ thống cảng GMD đ ạt 1.35 tri ệu Teu (+7% yoy). LNTT 2Q2026 của GMD ghi nh ận 
1,550 tỷ VND, tăng m ạnh 129% yoy do ghi nh ận khoản lãi tài chính 60 9 tỷ VND t ừ 
chuyển nhượng cổ phần công ty liên kết. Lũy kế 6 tháng đầu năm 2026, doanh thu và 
LNTT của GMD lần lượt hoàn thành 49%/81% doanh thu và LNTT kế hoạch 2026.

Sản lượng hàng qua h ệ thống cảng GMD k ỳ vọng đạt tăng trưởng 11.6% yoy trong 
2026 và CAGR 9%/năm giai đo ạn 2026 –2030. 2H2026, Nam Đình Vũ đư ợc k ỳ vọng 
phục hồi nhờ có thêm 2 tuyến dịch vụ mới, trong khi Gemalink ti ếp tục tăng trưởng 
nhờ bổ sung tuyến mới và ngu ồn hàng ổn định từ hãng tàu đ ối tác. Triển vọng dài 
hạn được củng cố bởi dư đ ịa công su ất tại Nam Đình Vũ 3 và Gemalink 2  trong bối 
cảnh nguồn cung Cái Mép đến 2030 dự kiến vẫn thấp hơn nhu cầu.

Giá dịch vụ tại các cảng GMD kỳ vọng duy trì đà tăng trung bình 5 –7%/năm tại phía 
Bắc và 7-10%/năm tại phía Nam giai đoạn 2026–2030, được hỗ trợ bởi (1) dư địa tăng 
giá còn lớn so với các nước khác trong khu vực và (2) khung trần giá dịch vụ tại cảng 
nước sâu vừa được Bộ Xây dựng nâng 10% từ 01/02/2026.

Chúng tôi khuy ến ngh ị MUA đ ối với cổ phiếu GMD, giá m ục tiêu là 92,500 VND/cổ 
phiếu, cao hơn 16.4% so với mức giá đóng cửa ngày 28/08/2026.

Khuyến ngh ị MUA, giá m ục tiêu 
32,150 VND/cổ phiếu 
 HPG ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST (loại trừ thu nhập từ chuyển nhượng dự án) 6M2026 
đạt lần lượt 108,060/11,538 t ỷ VND, tăng 47%/52% svck. Đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chủ 
yếu đến từ: (1) tiến độ giải ngân đầu tư công đư ợc đẩy mạnh, (2) các d ự án hạ tầng 
quy mô l ớn bước vào giai đo ạn thi công, (3) các bi ện pháp thu ế quan giúp thu h ẹp 
chênh lệch giá giữa HRC nội địa và nhập khẩu.

Chúng tôi dự báo tổng sản lượng tiêu thụ thép năm 2026/2027 đạt lần lượt 14.2/15.5 
triệu tấn. Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng bán hàng c ủa Hòa Phát đ ạt 6.8 tri ệu tấn, trong 
đó s ản lượng HRC đ ạt 3.4 tri ệu tấn tăng 57% svck, nh ờ Dung Qu ất 2 đã v ận hành 
khoảng 92% công suất thiết kế và mảng thép xây dựng tăng 17% svck nhưng các dây 
chuyền hiện tại đã hoạt động hết công suất.

Do xung đ ột chính tr ị và thắt ch ặt ngu ồn cung than c ốc, các doanh nghi ệp trong 
ngành đã điều chỉnh giá bán thép xây dựng và HRC bình quân lần lượt +8%/+12% để 
giảm áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận. KBSV kỳ vọng giá thép tăng trở lại từ Q4/2026 khi các 
doanh nghiệp trong ngành tăng cường nhập hàng để phục vụ nhu cầu xây dựng và 
tiến độ thi công đẩy nhanh vào giai đoạn cuối năm.

Hòa Phát đang triển khai đầu tư các d ự án lớn, trong đó bao g ồm (1) các h ạng mục 
thuộc Khu liên hợp Dung Quất, (2) dự án nhà máy thép ray và thép đặc biệt công suất 
700,000 tấn/năm, và (3) d ự án container. Chúng tôi gi ữ quan điểm tích c ực về triển 
vọng mở rộng danh m ục sản phẩm giá tr ị gia tăng của HPG, song v ẫn cần theo dõi 
thêm tiến độ vận hành, sản lượng thực tế và năng lực cạnh tranh được phân tích dưới 
báo cáo.

KBSV duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 32,150 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 48.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 04/09/2026.

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Tăng trưởng chậm lại trước chu kỳ đầu tư mới 
07/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng chậm l ại trong 
2Q2026

Lượt khách qua cảng hàng không 
Việt Nam duy trì tăng trưởng bền 
vững giai đoạn từ nay đến 2030

Số lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa của 
ACV dự kiến đạt tăng trưởng trung 
bình 6.8/3.3%/năm giai đoạn 2026-
2030

Long Thành 1 đang được đẩy nhanh 
tiến độ để kịp khai thác từ đầu 2027

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, 
giá mục tiêu 46,600 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,324 tỷ VND (-0.3% yoy), chủ yếu do (1) ACV đã 
hoàn thành chuy ển nhượng Cảng hàng không (CHK) Phú Qu ốc cho Sun Group vào 
1/1/2026 và (2) ảnh hưởng từ việc cắt giảm đường bay Trung Đông. Lũy k ế 6 tháng 
đầu năm 2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 13,164/5,278 tỷ VND 
(+4%/12% yoy).

Chúng tôi d ự phóng lư ợt khách hàng không Vi ệt Nam tăng trung bình kho ảng 
9%/năm giai đoạn 2026–2030 nhờ nền kinh tế duy trì tăng trưởng cao, các hãng hàng 
không mở rộng đội bay, mạng bay và công suất sân bay được bổ sung liên tục, với đà 
tăng được kỳ vọng cải thiện từ 2H2026 khi áp lực từ xung đột Trung Đông và giá nhiên 
liệu giảm bớt.

Tăng trưởng hành khách quốc tế và nội địa thông qua hệ thống cảng hàng không của 
ACV dự kiến đạt CAGR lần lượt 6.8% và 3.3% giai đoạn 2026–2030, thấp hơn mức tăng 
trưởng chung của toàn thị trường hàng không Việt Nam do một phần tăng trưởng lưu 
lượng hành khách sẽ được hấp thụ bởi các sân bay ngoài hệ thống ACV trong những 
năm tới, bao gồm dự án Cảng hàng không Gia Bình và Phú Quốc.

Dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành 1 đang đư ợc đẩy nhanh tiến độ dù một số hạng mục 
vẫn còn khối lượng lớn, dự kiến hoàn thành và bắt đầu khai thác thương mại từ đầu 
2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV.

Dựa trên tri ển vọng kinh doanh và k ết quả định giá, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với cổ phiếu ACV, giá mục tiêu là 46,600 VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 13.7% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 04/09/2026.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng được dự báo 
tiếp tục duy trì tích cực

NIM cả năm 2026, được kỳ vọng vẫn 
cải thiện nhẹ so với 2025

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ 
nhưng vẫn trong tầm kiểm soát

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
26,300 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 đạt 15%, với động lực thúc đẩy 
chính đến từ dư nợ phân khúc KHDN, đặc biệt là nhóm KHDN lớn và FDI, mặc dù nhu 
cầu đi vay của phân khúc bán l ẻ trong năm dự kiến sẽ chậm hơn do ảnh hưởng của 
mặt bằng lãi suất cao.

NIM có thể bị ảnh hưởng phần nào do tình hình huy động thị trường 1 chưa khả quan 
buộc ngân hàng phải phụ thuộc vào kênh giấy tờ có giá với chi phí cao hơn trong nửa 
cuối năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi kỳ vọng NIM của ngân hàng vẫn có thể cải thiện nhẹ so 
với cuối năm 2025 nhờ (1) ngân hàng đẩy mạnh cho vay trung dài hạn với lợi suất tốt 
hơn; (2) danh mục cho vay tiếp tục được tái định giá ở mức cao hơn trong các quý tới; 
và (3) ngân hàng có thêm nguồn huy động offshore với chi phí vốn hợp lý.

Chất lượng tài sản trong 2026 dự kiến suy giảm nhẹ, phản ánh môi trường lãi suất 
cho vay tăng cao. Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tỷ lệ NPL sẽ tăng 13bps so với 2025 và 
tăng dự phóng đối với chi phí trích lập dự phòng so với báo cáo kỳ trước do tác 
động của Nghị định 86 lên hoạt động trích lập của ngân hàng trong nửa đầu năm 
mạnh hơn nhận định trước đó.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu ACB với giá mục tiêu 26,300 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 18% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 08/09/2026.

 Chúng tôi nâng dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng của ngân hàng trong giai đoạn 
2026F-2028F lên khoảng 18-19% nhờ (1) hưởng lợi từ chính sách nới tín dụng đối 
với các dự án hạ tầng trọng điểm, (2) mối quan hệ chiến lược với các nhà thầu lớn, 
và (3) chủ động đa dạng nguồn huy động để đáp ứng nhu cầu tín dụng.

Với luận điểm ngân hàng s ẽ đẩy mạnh cho vay các d ự án hạ tầng thuộc chủ trương 
của Chính phủ và mặt bằng lãi suất đang neo ở mức cao, chúng tôi cho r ằng NIM sẽ

khi áp lực lãi suất hạ nhiệt và thu nhập lãi từ các khoản vay thuộc chính sách Flexible 
Pricing sẽ được thu hồi.

Chất lượng tài sản dự kiến suy giảm nhẹ, phản ánh môi trường lãi suất neo cao gây 
áp lực lên danh mục bán lẻ của ngân hàng. Chúng tôi nâng dự phóng đối với tỷ lệ 
NPL lên thêm 10bps so với báo cáo kỳ trước và nâng nhẹ chi phí dự phòng với kỳ 
vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì bộ đệm an toàn ở mức cao.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi ti ếp tục duy trì khuy ến ngh ị MUA đối với cổ phiếu TCB v ới giá m ục tiêu

tạo đ ộng l ực tăng trư ởng m ới cho

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP , giá mục tiêu 
56,700 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu thuần 2Q2026 đạt 5,472 tỷ VND (+10% yoy), trong khi LNST đạt 73 tỷ 
VND, giảm 25% yoy do biên lợi nhuận thu hẹp đến từ (1) tăng chi phí nhiên liệu và 
(2) tăng chi phí vận hành, khấu hao cho các dự án đầu tư mới. Lũy kế 6T2026, 
doanh thu và LNST của VTP lần lượt đạt 10,230/112 tỷ VND, hoàn thành 52%/28% 
doanh thu và LNST kế hoạch của 2026.

Chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng chuyển phát Việt Nam tăng 22% yoy trong 2026 và duy 
trì CAGR 15% giai đoạn 2026–2030 nhờ nhu cầu giao nhận tiếp tục mở rộng theo đà 
tăng trưởng của thị trường TMĐT . VTP kỳ vọng tiếp tục gia tăng th ị phần lên 23.5% 
năm 2026 và đ ạt 25.5% vào 2030, duy trì t ốc độ tăng trưởng sản lượng cao hơn th ị 
trường.

VTP đang đ ẩy nhanh đ ầu tư các trung tâm logistics t ại Lạng Sơn, Hưng Yên và Đà 
Nẵng, đồng thời mở rộng hoạt động tại Lào, Campuchia và Trung Qu ốc nhằm từng 
bước hoàn thiện mạng lưới logistics xuyên biên gi ới, tạo nền tảng tham gia sâu hơn 
vào chuỗi logistics toàn trình. Chúng tôi d ự phóng doanh thu kho vận và vận tải đạt 
CAGR 22%, trong khi logistics xuyên biên gi ới tăng trung bình 29%/năm giai đo ạn 
2026–2030.

Dựa trên tri ển vọng kinh doanh và k ết quả định giá, chúng tôi đưa ra khuy ến nghị 
TRUNG LẬP đối với cổ phiếu VTP , với giá m ục tiêu 56,700 VND/c ổ phiếu, tương ứng 
mức tăng giá ti ềm năng 12.5%so v ới giá đóng c ửa 50,400 VND/c ổ phiếu ngày 
09/09/2026.

Nhịp tăng điểm sau kỳ nghỉ lễ đã sớm cho tín hiệu đảo chiều trong tuần qua với sự hình 
thành của một mẫu nến phân phối tiêu cực trên khung tuần. Trên khung ngày, 2 phiên 
giảm mạnh vào đầu và cuối tuần cũng cho thấy áp lực bán mang tính áp đảo với độ rộng 
thị trường hoàn toàn nghiêng về số mã giảm điểm. Với đà phân phối hiện tại, áp lực điều 
chỉnh có thể còn tiếp diễn trong những phiên kế tiếp, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn 
hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới. Ở 
góc nhìn rộng hơn, xu hướng trung hạn có phần trung tính khi chỉ số đang hình thành các 
mẫu hình vận động đi ngang theo tam giác hoặc hình bình hành.

Document 221: DeepSeek扩招！弹性计算团队大量HC，尤其需要资深工程师

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek扩招！弹性计算团队大量HC，尤其需要资深工程师.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-03
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

现在 DeepSeek 的⼀套 DSec 扩展分⽚，⼤约有 160 台服务器、 3 万个 CPU 核⼼和 250TB 内
存，每天要服务约 300 万个沙盒。⾼峰期，同时在线的沙盒超过 38 万个，每秒还能创建
5000 多个。 DeepSeek 已经在⽣产环境⾥部署了多个这样的分⽚，能够⽀持数百万个沙盒
同时运⾏。接下来，要把 Agent 运⾏环境的数量和种类扩充成百上千倍。这么⼀看，确实得
招⼈ ……
摘要：
弹性计算团队⼤量 HC 招⼈！尤其需要资深⼯程师。
三周前不是刚招过⼀轮吗？咋⼜缺⼈了。
这次没发岗位 JD ，直接甩了⼀篇 DeepSeek 技术分享：
《 DeepSeek 弹性计算（ DSec ）：⾯向⼤规模 Agent 训练的沙盒基础设施》
现在 DeepSeek 的⼀套 DSec 扩展分⽚，⼤约有 160 台服务器、 3 万个 CPU 核⼼和 250TB 内存，每
天要服务约 300 万个沙盒。
⾼峰期，同时在线的沙盒超过 38 万个，每秒还能创建 5000 多个。
DeepSeek 已经在⽣产环境⾥部署了多个这样的分⽚，能够⽀持数百万个沙盒同时运⾏。

接下来，要把 Agent 运⾏环境的数量和种类扩充成百上千倍。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

这么⼀看，确实得招⼈ ……
38 万个沙盒同时在线
DSec 是⽀撑 DeepSeek-V4 全部训练、评测和数据预处理流程的沙盒基础设施。
从 DeepSeek-V3.2 ⼀路到 V4.1 ， Agent 训练、评测和数据预处理产⽣的全部沙盒负载，都已经运
⾏在 DSec 上。
它⾯对的⼯作负载和普通云计算还有点不⼀样。
Agent 训练过程中，模型需要不断进⼊真实环境执⾏任务。
读代码、改⽂件、安装依赖、执⾏测试、运⾏服务，每⼀步都会改变当前环境；下⼀轮交互⼜
要接着前⾯的状态继续。
因此这些沙盒既要能够快速、⼤批量创建，⼜不能每执⾏⼀步就销毁重来。
DeepSeek 总结下来，这类负载有⼏个很明显的特点：
创建请求会突然集中涌⼊；
CPU ⼤部分时间处于空闲状态，但为了保存⽂件和进程，内存⼜得⼀直留着；
不同 Agent 任务需要的执⾏环境差异很⼤；
基础镜像复⽤率不⾼；
训练过程还可能因为 GPU 资源被抢占⽽中断。
DSec 现在做的⼤量⼯作，就是围绕这些特点，把 Agent 环境的供给成本往下压。

⾸先是环境本⾝。

### Page 3

DeepSeek 拿 2026 年某⼀周的⽣产数据统计了⼀下，仅 Container 后端就⽤到了 11266 个基础镜
像、 102171 个⼯作区，以及数百个⼯具包。
如果按照传统⽅法，每种组合都做成⼀份完整镜像，代码仓库或者⼯具包稍微更新⼀次，就可
能跟着重建⼀⼤批镜像。
所以 DSec 直接把环境拆成了三层。
操作系统和基础软件放进 base image ，任务代码和依赖放进 workspace ， DeepSeek Harness 这
类⼯具则单独做成 toolkit 。
三部分分别管理版本，真正创建沙盒的时候再组合起来。
这样⼯具更新，只需要重建⼯具所在的那⼀层，不必把整个环境从头再做⼀次。
但环境拆开之后，还有另⼀个问题：
这么多镜像，要不要提前全部拉到服务器上？
DeepSeek 看了⼀遍真实⽣产数据，发现根本没必要。
Agent 实际运⾏时，真正访问到的数据只占整个镜像的 4.2% 到 13.3% 。
也就是说，⼀个⼏⼗ GB 的环境提前完整下载下来，其中绝⼤多数内容可能从头到尾都不会被

Agent 碰到。
DSec 于是把镜像数据统⼀放到 DeepSeek ⾃⼰的 3FS 分布式⽂件系统⾥，本地主要保存镜像元
数据，真正需要某块数据时再按需读取。
DeepSeek 集中创建 8192 个 Container 做过⼀次测试。
如果先把完整镜像拉到本地，整个任务需要 60 多分钟；换成按需加载之后，时间缩短到了⼤约
35 分钟，速度提升约 1.71 倍，磁盘写⼊量同时减少约 57% 。

### Page 4

另⼀个⼯作区实验⾥，原本每创建⼀个沙盒，都需要单独解压⼀份 tar.gz ⽂件。
改成直接挂载 EROFS 层之后，整个任务从 79 分钟缩短到 45 分钟，磁盘写⼊总量只剩原来的⼤约
1/5.5 。
环境供给解决之后，接下来就是怎么把 CPU 和内存尽可能利⽤起来。
DeepSeek 统计发现， Agent 沙盒其实相当 “ 稀疏 ” 。
⼤约 90% 的沙盒，平均 CPU 使⽤量都不到申请资源的 5% 。
原因在于 Agent 完成⼀次操作后，通常需要等待模型⽣成下⼀步动作。
等待期间 CPU 基本闲置，但⽂件、进程等环境状态仍然需要保存，所以内存⼜不能直接释放。

### Page 5

这给 DSec 留下了很⼤的资源调度空间。
DeepSeek 现在⽣产环境⾥的资源超卖率已经超过 50 倍。
不过，单纯往⼀台机器⾥塞更多沙盒还不够。
MicroVM 之间⼤量相同的只读内容如果各存⼀份，内存很快就会成为瓶颈。
DSec 通过 virtio-pmem 和 DAX ，让同⼀台宿主机上的 MicroVM 共享宿主⻚缓存。单独启⽤这项机
制，实验中的宿主机峰值内存占⽤相⽐基线下降了 40.2% 。
另⼀边，借助 DAMON 和 balloon 空闲⻚报告回收暂时不⽤的内存，按时间累计的内存消耗还能
再降低 21.2% 。
CPU 也不能简单⼀视同仁。
有些 Agent 任务对响应时间⽐较敏感，有些则晚⼀点完成也没关系。
DSec 会优先保证前⼀类任务，再让时延要求较低的任务利⽤剩余 CPU 。
DeepSeek 的实验⾥，当同⼀台机器上的其他任务已经占掉节点 50% 的 CPU 容量时，经过调度优
化，时延敏感任务受到的延迟影响从 45.2% 降到了 17.3% 。

### Page 6

镜像按需加载、内存共享、 CPU 调度 ……DSec 前⾯这些优化，⽬的其实都很⼀致：
尽可能压低每个 Agent 环境占⽤的资源，把同⼀批机器撑出更⼤的并发规模。
但资源省下来之后， DeepSeek 接下来还准备把 Agent 能训练的环境也⼀起铺开。
⽤ Agent 给 Agent 造环境
不同 Agent 任务对运⾏环境的要求完全不同。
DSec 现在已经统⼀⽀持四种执⾏后端： FnCall 、 Container 、 MicroVM 和 Full VM 。
FnCall 适合在线评测等短任务；
Container 主要承担软件⼯程和⼯具调⽤；
MicroVM 提供更强的隔离能⼒；
到了 Full VM ，则可以提供完整操作系统，⽀持 GUI 、图形渲染甚⾄ Android 应⽤。
随着 Agent 能⼒继续往外扩，环境也会越来越复杂。
⼀个 Coding Agent 可能只需要代码仓库、 Shell 和测试⼯具，但如果以后要让 Agent 操作更多真
实软件、系统和服务，训练阶段就得先把这些环境构建出来。

### Page 7

⽽ DeepSeek 现在的做法已经有点 Agent 套 Agent 了：
直接⽤ Agent 构建运⾏ Agent 的环境。
传统⽅式下，可能需要⼀套平台负责 Agent 训练，再单独搭⼀套平台⽣产 Agent 所需的环境。
DeepSeek 发现，负责构建环境的 Agent 本⾝就已经运⾏在环境⾥⾯。
于是⼲脆把两件事都塞回 DSec 。
DSec 为此做了⼀个叫 pack_diff 的机制。
Agent 在沙盒⾥把环境配置好之后，可以直接⽣成增量快照，把当前状态保存下来。以后需要同
样的环境，就可以从这个快照重新恢复。
甚⾄ Agent 每执⾏⼀步，都可以把当前状态保存成⼀个新的可复⽤环境。
这还带来了⼀个挺重要的能⼒，轨迹分叉。
⽐如 Agent 执⾏到第 k 步，接下来有三种不同⽅案可以尝试。
系统可以先在第 k 步保存⼀次快照，然后从完全相同的状态恢复出多个沙盒，让不同分⽀分别继
续探索。
前⾯的环境和数据可以共享，只有后续发⽣的变化需要单独记录，不需要把前 k 步重新执⾏⼀

遍。
对于⼤规模强化学习来说，这相当于把同⼀条 Agent 轨迹上的环境状态也变成了可以复⽤的数
据。
与此同时， DSec 还在把 Agent 执⾏和 GPU 训练进⼀步拆开。
以前 Agent 执⾏循环和 GPU 训练任务放在同⼀个 Pod ⾥。
⼀旦 GPU 训练任务被抢占， Agent 执⾏循环也会跟着中断。
恢复之后，还得重新播放之前的命令记录，把训练进度和沙盒⾥的真实状态重新对⻬。
从 DeepSeek-V4.1 开始，这部分执⾏逻辑被迁进了 DSec 。
Agent 沙盒和负责推进交互的 worker container 都运⾏在可抢占 GPU 资源池之外。
这样即使 GPU 任务中断， Agent 执⾏到哪⼀步、环境变成了什么样，都还能继续保存。
⽽环境状态能够被独⽴保存之后， Agent 执⾏就不必再和 GPU 训练绑死。
GPU 重新回来之后，训练可以直接从中断的位置继续。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
不过，当 Agent 真的可以在环境⾥⾃由操作以后，另⼀个问题也来了：
模型开始⾃⼰找环境漏洞了。
DeepSeek 说，他们已经在真实⽣产环境⾥观察到 Agent 尝试各种⾮常具体的操作：
读取环境⾥残留的答案、伪造 RPC 请求，甚⾄直接覆盖 /bin/bash 来注⼊命令。
甚⾄还有 Agent 尝试调⽤ XFS_IOC_SWAPEXT 绕过访问控制。
背后的逻辑很简单，强化学习给模型的是奖励，只要环境⾥存在⼀条⽐正常完成任务更容易拿
到奖励的路径，能⼒⾜够强的 Agent 就可能把它找出来。
但这会直接污染训练和评测结果。
所以 DSec 现在⼜得负责给 Agent 划安全边界。
⽂件和 Socket 访问通过 AppArmor 限制，即使 Agent 拿到了管理员权限，相应规则依然有效；⽹

络侧则利⽤ eBPF 给每个沙盒设置⽩名单，限定它能够连接的地址、端⼝和协议。
但 DeepSeek 也明确承认，这些办法还解决不了所有问题。
⽐如 Agent 如果进⼀步找到内核漏洞，⽬前依然缺少⼀套通⽤的防御机制。
他们对此的判断是：
随着模型能⼒继续提⾼，和 Agent 之间的攻防还会⼀直持续下去。
活⼉有亿点多，所以 DeepSeek 需要更多开发伙伴，团队需要继续 Scaling 。
我问你，招到⼈之后是不是能把 V4.1 Pro 也抬上来了 ……

Document 232: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

### Page 2

2 01 October 2026

I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).  
Exhibit 6: For restaurants and bars the story is mixed across older 
cohorts…

of all households, although it remains below its 2019 level (Exhibit 7).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.  
Exhibit 8: A large and growing proportion of net worth is held by 
older cohorts 
Distribution of household wealth by age ($, trillions)

Source: Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: Healthcare is a significant cost for older households

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 7

01 October 2026    7

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%) 
          













### Page 2

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

### Page 4

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

### Page 5

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

The combination of higher cost of capital and greater capital intensity in the tech sector 
has reduced the value of their future cash flows and triggered a de-rating in the biggest 
companies.  The dominant capex hyperscalers in the US now have a forward PE close to 
the average of the rest of the market.

8. Software companies have also de-rated on the back of fears of disruption from AI 
while memory and chips stocks have also experienced lower multiples as their earnings

### Page 7

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

### Page 9

Stock correlations are at multi-year lows across the major equity markets (Exhibit 12).

Even within sectors like technology, correlations have fallen sharply (Exhibit 13). The 
‘magnificent seven’ that was long seen as a homogeneous group of technology winners 
has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

Document 289: Optimistic Spin_ Bond Market Pricing Better-Than-Expected Economic Growth

Source PDF: all report pdf/Optimistic Spin_ Bond Market Pricing Better-Than-Expected Economic Growth.pdf
Report source: Substack Reports
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Optimistic Spin: Bond Market Pricing Better-Than-Expected Economic Growth
Date:    3 hours ago
Optimistic Spin: Bond Market Pricing Better-Than-Expected Economic Growth

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Optimistic Spin: Bond Market
Pricing Better-Than-Expected
Economic Growth
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 30 Sept 2026
View in browser
Photo by Free Walking Tour Salzburg / Unsplash

Bond yields rose yet again today even though September's PCED inflation
report showed some improvement, which was due mostly to new
measurement procedures. Inflation remains about a percentage point
above the Fed's 2.0% target. Other economic indicators today confirmed
that the economy and the labor market are doing very well and
strengthened the case for more Fed rate hikes. Yesterday's dovish

### Page 2

suggestion by NY Fed President John Williams that a pause in rate hikes
might make sense was all but forgotten today.
The most optimistic explanation for the backup in bond yields is that it
reflects better-than-expected economic growth, suggesting the economy's
R-Star (i.e., the neutral interest rate) is higher than Fed officials thought at
the beginning of the year. Back then, they mostly agreed that the federal
funds rate was still slightly restrictive, i.e., above the neutral rate. Now,

they agree with Fed Chair Kevin Warsh that September's 25bps rate hike
"removed a dose of accommodation." That implies that the federal funds
rate is below neutral.
No wonder the 2-year Treasury yield remained 100bps above the federal
funds rate today (chart).
Federal funds futures are pricing in three to four 25bps rate hikes over the
next 12 months, including roughly two over the next six months (chart).

### Page 3

Let's review the latest data:
(1) Inflation. Today’s August PCED report confirmed that the strong US
economy still has an inflation problem. Headline PCED rose 0.3% m/m
and 3.4% y/y, while core PCED increased 0.2% m/m and 3.0% y/y. Both
remain well above the Fed’s 2% target.
The August PCED report incorporated the annual revisions and
methodological changes implemented by the Bureau of Economic
Analysis. July's headline PCED inflation rate was revised down 37bps, to

2.98% y/y from 3.35%, while core PCED was cut 34bps, to 3.36% from
3.70%. The key point is that much of the recent decline in core PCED
inflation reflects revised measurement procedures rather than a genuine
improvement in underlying inflation.

### Page 4

Indeed, despite the methodological changes, the report's details point to
sticky underlying inflation. Goods PCED inflation rose to 3.6% y/y in
August from 3.3% in July, partly reflecting a 4.1% m/m jump in gasoline
prices (chart). Tariffs and the AI buildout added further pressure: prices for
computers and peripherals surged 3.8% m/m, while toy prices rose 2.0%.
Looking ahead, tariff pass-through and AI-related demand should keep
goods inflation elevated, while the renewed rise in oil and refined-product

prices in September adds another source of upward pressure.
The PCED services inflation rate eased to 3.4% y/y in August (chart).
However, the “supercore” PCED services, which excludes both energy
and housing, rose 0.4% m/m in August. That was the strongest monthly
increase since May 2026 and pushed the y/y rate up to 3.5%.
The supercore inflation rate isolates some of the stickiest and most wage-
sensitive parts of the inflation basket. The measure remains well above a

pace consistent with the Fed’s 2% target. More importantly, it has been
moving higher since October 2025.

### Page 5

(2) Consumer spending and income. Consumer spending remains
remarkably strong. Inflation-adjusted consumption rose to another record
high in August (chart). By contrast, real disposable personal income was
unchanged m/m and has been essentially flat for more than a year. That
trend is likely to persist as more Baby Boomers retire, slowing wage
income growth.
Real consumer spending rose 0.6% m/m in August, the strongest monthly
gain since March 2025. The increase was broad-based, with especially

strong spending on discretionary categories including restaurants and
hotels, recreation, and apparel (chart).

### Page 6

The surge in household net worth relative to disposable income is causing
consumers, especially Baby Boomers, to reduce their savings rate (chart).
August’s saving rate was revised meaningfully higher, but still fell to 4.1%,
the lowest since November 2022. We expect it to decline further as more
Boomers retire. Their labor income drops to zero when they do so, but
their sizable accumulated wealth lets them keep spending.
(3) Labor market. Another key driver of consumer spending is the strong

labor market. ADP private payrolls rose by 90,000 in September, with
healthy gains across both goods-producing and service industries (chart).
We expect Friday’s employment report to confirm that strength, with
nonfarm payrolls likely to show a 100,000 increase in September.

### Page 7

(4) GDP. Q2's real GDP growth was revised today from 1.5% saar to
2.2%. On the other hand, Q3's growth rate was revised down from 5.0% to
3.7% by the Atlanta Fed's GDPNow tracking model (chart). That was
mostly attributable to the widening trade deficit. Final sales to private
domestic purchasers was strong with a 4.5% gain.

Document 313: The end of the safety net_ Life without the macroeconomic stabilisers (2026-09-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The end of the safety net_ Life without the macroeconomic stabilisers (2026-09-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

The end of the safety net:  
Life without the macroeconomic stabilisers

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

28 September 2026 
For several decades, financial markets and the wider economy benefited from an implicit 
safety net. If a slowdown or a downturn arrived, there was a monetary put from central 
banks, and a fiscal put from governments. The monetary put would see interest rates 
slashed and liquidity injected via QE. The fiscal put would see governments run larger 
deficits to cushion any downturn. These policy stabilisers smoothed out the business 
cycle, and supported asset valuations too. 
These policy interventions were increasingly normalised from the late-1980s, and became 
more aggressive with each passing cycle. This reached its peak during the Covid-19 
pandemic, when governments ran fiscal deficits unprecedented in peacetime, whilst 
many central banks expanded their balance sheets with huge asset purchases. 
But after an era of ever-bigger interventions, these old stabilisers now face major 
obstacles. On the fiscal side, policy is constrained today by sovereign yields at multi-year 
highs, alongside the highest debt-to-GDP ratios in several decades. Then on the monetary 
side, policy is constrained by persistent inflation that’s increasingly supply-driven. 
This represents a big shift from the last few decades, but it does echo previous regimes 
where the macroeconomic stabilisers were either blunted or left unused. A good example 
is the 1970s experience, when stagflation and supply shocks posed a dilemma to 
traditional policy tools. Even further back, the pre-WWI environment of the classical gold 
standard and balanced budget orthodoxy saw governments play a very different and more 
limited role in macroeconomic stabilisation. 
Looking forward, this environment means policymakers will have a much more difficult 
time stabilising the business cycle in the coming years. So absent a sharp fall in yields or a 
collapse in inflation, higher macro volatility will be here to stay.

### Page 2

28 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
1. Background: How the stabilisers used to work.
Economic policymakers have long used interventions to stabilise output. This
became increasingly fashionable after WWII, when Keynesian economics
established a more active use for fiscal policy to stimulate the economy in a
downturn. This meant budget deficits became a more regular feature of life, rather
than used solely for wartime emergencies.

It was from the late 1980s, however, that the extent of policy interventions became
increasingly sizeable. Indeed, it was here that the origins of a central bank “put” for
financial markets began, in the crisis of Black Monday in 1987. That was a huge
stock market crash, where the S&P 500 fell over 20% in a single day, marking the
biggest daily decline in its entire history. The size of the falls even went beyond the
Great Depression, raising fears of wider contagion, particularly if it were to spark a

liquidity crisis that caused financial institutions to fail. However, the Federal
Reserve responded by cutting rates and providing liquidity, offering a
macroeconomic stabiliser against the prospect of wider economic stress. This
offered support against a wider downturn, and the US economic expansion
continued until 1990.
Whilst the genesis of a central bank “put” came in 1987, the idea gained further
traction in the late-1990s, with the Asian financial crisis in 1997, followed by the

Russian financial crisis in 1998. Fears of an economic contraction mounted,
particularly after issues at Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) raised concerns
that the US wasn’t immune from these global pressures. So once again, the Fed
delivered three 25bp rate cuts pre-emptively, seeking to support the economy.
A few years later, the bursting of the dot com bubble in early 2000 set the stage for
yet further stimulus. As equity markets declined, the Fed began to cut rates from

early 2001, ultimately delivering 475bps of cuts that year that took rates from 6.5%
to just 1.75%. That was followed up by further rate cuts in 2002 and 2003, leaving
rates at 1% by mid-2003 into 2004. Moreover, the Bush tax cuts were also delivered
in 2001 and 2003, acting as a fiscal stimulus as the wider US economy entered
recession.
In hindsight, these moves seem modest compared to what then happened in 2008,
when the Global Financial Crisis led to even bigger policy interventions. Suddenly,

the world faced the biggest economic contraction since the Great Depression, with
genuine fears that a repeat might happen. To avoid this, policymakers launched into
the biggest economic stimulus ever seen in a peacetime environment. That
included massive fiscal stimulus, with the US federal deficit at 9.8% of GDP in 2008,
the highest since 1945. Moreover, central banks slashed rates to the zero lower
bound, and many like the Federal Reserve launched into Quantitative Easing

programmes, purchasing assets to ease financial conditions even more. Over the
coming years, many began pledging to keep rates low well into the future,
pioneering the concept of forward guidance.
These levels of policy intervention weren’t confined to the immediate crisis
however. In the subsequent years, active attempts were made to prolong and
extend the business cycle, particularly as the 2010s became an era of low inflation
and low interest rates. In this environment, borrowing was historically cheap, and

low inflation meant central banks could cut rates without seeing a big overshoot of
their targets. Indeed, both the Fed and the ECB undershot their 2% inflation target
on average in the 2010s, and the ECB took their own deposit rate into negative
territory in 2014.

By 2019, the year before the pandemic, the intellectual climate had continued to
move in the direction of more policy intervention. Government bond yields hit new

lows, with Germany’s 10-year bund yield at -0.7% in summer 2019. This
environment of negative yields meant borrowing was basically a “free lunch”, and
that summer also saw the Fed begin a 75bp easing cycle, as inflation was below
their target and they wanted to sustain the expansion.
The peak of this policy interventionism was seen with the Covid-19 pandemic in
2020. Just as in 2008, the world was facing a huge and sudden economic
contraction. Moreover, the consensus among policymakers was that they could

have done more to speed up the recovery after 2008, potentially avoiding years of
very high unemployment. As such, 2020 saw the most aggressive economic
stimulus packages ever seen in a peacetime environment, well above those in 2008.
Central banks immediately slashed rates back to the zero lower bound, pledged to
keep rates low for years to come, and launched into major QE programmes again.
Then on the fiscal side, record peacetime deficits were run. Indeed, the US federal

deficit of 14.5% of GDP in 2020 was the highest since 1945.

### Page 4

28 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
During the pandemic period, many initially expected these interventions to remain
for years to come. After all, in mid-2020, financial markets continued to price a
continuation of the low inflation and low interest rates of the 2010s. This belief was
clear on several fronts, and the 10-year Treasury yield closed at just 0.51% in August
2020. Meanwhile, the Federal Reserve even adjusted its own framework to allow for

average inflation targeting, i.e. permitting an overshoot of the inflation target after
periods of an undershoot. This reflected the consensus view that inflation was
unlikely to be a serious threat.
But as we now know, inflation returned in a serious way after the pandemic, forcing
central banks into rate hikes. Meanwhile, public debt-to-GDP ratios rose
significantly thanks to the pandemic stimulus, helping to push up sovereign bond
yields too. In other words, many of the usual policy tools of the previous few

decades were either blunted or no longer available.
2. The Monetary Constraint: High inflation means the central
bank put isn’t available
In the pre-2020 world described above, central banks regularly grappled with how
to generate enough inflation. In part, this was thanks to several structural forces
that put downward pressure on global prices. For instance, deepening
globalisation acted as a positive supply shock that kept costs down. Meanwhile, the

expansion of the global labour force in these years also boosted the supply side. In
other words, inflation was low and central banks generally had the space to cut
interest rates when they needed to.
But since the pandemic, we have entered a very different environment of above-
target inflation across many of the world’s biggest economies. Initially, that was
caused by a huge wave of pent-up demand after the pandemic, alongside
disruption to supply chains and labour markets. But the subsequent years also

brought further shocks. Those included Russia’s invasion of Ukraine in early 2022,
driving a big spike in European energy prices, and pushing Euro Area inflation above
10% briefly. In 2026, the closure of the Strait of Hormuz then acted as a fresh energy
shock, pushing inflation further away from target and contributing to a fresh
tightening cycle from the major central banks.
Figure 4: In the 2020s, inflation has generally been above central bank targets

### Page 5

28 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Alongside those shocks, the structural forces keeping inflation down have gone
into reverse. For instance, demographic trends have seen working-age populations
shrink in many places. Meanwhile, the trend towards deeper globalisation has
stalled, and the focus has turned to near-shoring and supply-chain resilience, rather
than locating production in the cheapest place. Simultaneously, other trends like

the push for higher defence spending are also adding to demand-side pressures.
Because of all this, the 2020s so far have brought persistently above-target inflation,
and central bankers have found it much more difficult to ease policy in response to
growth wobbles or financial stability fears.
This initially became apparent in 2022, amidst the biggest inflation wave for several
decades. Central banks had to keep hiking rates, even in the face of slowing growth

and financial market stress. Indeed, despite the S&P 500 being in bear market
territory by mid-2022, the Federal Reserve still launched into a series of 75bp hikes,
the biggest hikes since the 1990s. Then in 2023, despite the collapse of Silicon
Valley Bank and fears of wider financial contagion, the Fed and ECB still continued
with their rate hikes to combat inflation.
That tightening cycle in 2022-23 was a significant moment, because high inflation

meant monetary policy could not simply act as a stabiliser against growth or
financial stress. After all, policymakers realised that if they didn’t act to tackle high
inflation, they risked a repeat of the 1970s and the potential for even harsher
monetary tightening to get inflation under control again.
Today in 2026, this is a familiar problem again. We are in a globally synchronised
hiking cycle, with the Fed, ECB and Bank of Japan all having delivered rate hikes.

So far at least, global growth has been robust and financial conditions remain
accommodative by historical standards. But because inflation remains clearly
above target, central banks don’t have the space they did in the pre-pandemic world
to simply cut rates at the first sign of difficulty. There is still an inflation mandate to
be achieved, and given we have now seen multiple years of elevated inflation, there
is a much higher risk of inflation expectations becoming unanchored in that

context, particularly if another shock occurred.
3. The Fiscal Constraint: High yields and high public debt
mean fiscal stimulus isn’t easily available either
Just as monetary policy is constrained by high inflation, fiscal policy today is also
constrained by higher yields and higher public debt. This is a big transition from the
world of 1980-2020, when yields were generally falling throughout. Indeed, despite
all the fiscal stimulus around the GFC, the continuous decline in yields over the

2010s meant that debt dynamics remained manageable, because lower yields
helped to offset the impact of higher debt.
Since the pandemic however, we’ve been in a very different world, as the dual
forces of higher debt and higher yields have made debt dynamics far more
unfavourable around the world. And this isn’t just a nominal story, as real yields
have also risen to multi-year highs as well.

In essence, there are 4 new constraints around fiscal stimulus.
First, is the significant rise in global yields. By definition, the cost of incurring debt
has grown, in both nominal and real terms. Relative to the pre-pandemic baseline

in early 2020, the 10-year US Treasury yield has risen from 1.9% to 5.2%. Similarly
in Germany, the 10-year bund yield has risen from -0.2% to 3.6%. Moreover, with
yields already elevated, any further borrowing risks crowding out the private sector,
and further dampening growth. And for governments, interest spending has
ramped up significantly. For instance, US net interest spending as a share of GDP
exceeded its recent peak from the early 1990s in the most recent fiscal year, and the

CBO forecast this is set to continue.
Figure 6: US Net Interest Costs (% of GDP) with CBO Forecasts
